# Full repository audit — PsychSift — 2026-09-02

**Status: audit only. Nothing in this document has been acted on, except the one-line documentation corrections listed in §11 and landed as a separate, labelled commit on the same branch.** No code, migration, script, workflow, gate, ledger table, or generated data file other than `data/repo-awareness-snapshot.json` changes on this branch.

## 1. Why this exists

The last whole-repository audit was 2026-07-01 (`docs/audit/repo-audit-2026-07-01.md`, 41 findings, all actioned by 2026-07-02), followed by the 2026-07-19 sweep and the 2026-07-23 remediation plan. Since then the repository has grown three gated prototypes (Ward Flow, Care Plan, the Caring Contacts mockup), a live Caring Contacts workspace with its own database, a dormant Clinical Ask orchestrator, roughly 80 design mockups, 224 migrations, 292 npm scripts and 1,032 test files. The owner asked for a meticulous audit of every part of the repository including the closed-off projects, using multiple subagents and every relevant skill, so that nothing is taken on trust.

## 2. How to read this

- **Severity** follows the 2026-07-01 audit: **High** = a clinician could act on a wrong number or a caveat-less answer, another owner's or a patient's data is reachable, remote code execution, secret exposure, or data loss; **Medium** = a defence-in-depth gap, silent degradation, a stale governance or safety claim, or wrong gate coverage; **Low** = quality, drift, hygiene.
- **Verdict** is the independent skeptic's, not the finder's: **CONFIRMED** (reproduced offline, or traced end to end with quoted code), **PLAUSIBLE** (credible trace but a runtime precondition could not be established offline — the precondition is named), **DUPLICATE** (already an open row in `docs/outstanding-issues.md` or a prior-audit item; listed under §9 "Corroborated pre-existing debts", with a note where the finder shows the row is now stale), **REFUTED** (listed in §13 with the guard that refuted it).
- Every finding that survived carries the skeptic's re-graded severity. Where the finder said High and the skeptic said Medium, Medium is what is shown.
- `safe_cleanup=true` means the suggested fix has zero behaviour change. Nothing in this document is a delete list: dead-looking code is a triage candidate with its evidence, per `docs/agents/dead-code-deletion.md`.

## 3. The evidence base and its limits — say it plainly

- **Verification was thinner than designed.** The first pass launched three independent skeptics per High/Medium finding (about 300 agents); that pass hit the session's usage limit after the finders had completed and only three votes were recorded. On the owner's instruction to preserve progress and conserve usage, verification was re-run as **one independent skeptic per lane group** (13 skeptics for 25 lanes), each opening every cited line, searching for existing guards, re-deriving the trigger, and checking the ledger, in a single pass. That is one adversarial opinion per finding rather than three. The critic and the Stage-5 review were each a single agent, so the red-team, blue-team and router sections in §15 come from one reviewer wearing three lenses, not three independent reviewers.
- **Three lanes were read after the others.** L03b (dashboard UI), L06 (Supabase schema and RLS) and L23 (route reachability) failed on the same usage limit and were re-run in the lean pass; their findings were verified by the same single-skeptic method.
- **Nothing provider-backed ran.** No OpenAI call, no live Supabase read, no Railway or Sentry call, no hosted CI dispatch. The Supabase, Railway and Sentry MCP servers were unauthenticated in this session and were not needed.
- **Not run, and why:** `check:drift` (needs a Docker daemon; none is reachable inside this container. Note the two gates are different: CI's `Migration replay` job replays the chain with `supabase migration up --local`, while `check:drift` itself runs only in the post-merge `live-drift` workflow); `build`, `check:client-bundle-secrets` and `check:bundle-budget` (need a production build; CI `build` job); `verify:ui`, `verify:phone-chrome` and all `test:e2e*` (browser suite; per this repository's testing policy the browser proof is left to CI, and `check:playwright-browser-revision` confirmed the pinned Chromium is not installed here); every `eval:*`, `verify:release`, `governance:release`, `check:production-readiness`, `check:supabase-project` (provider-backed and approval-gated; `check:production-readiness` also has an offline-tolerant `check:production-readiness:ci` variant, which was not run either).
- **Semgrep ran with the open-source rule repository, not CI's registry packs.** The proxy blocks `semgrep.dev`, so the `p/typescript` etc. packs could not be fetched; the `semgrep/semgrep-rules` repository (commit `40b8c63`) was cloned from GitHub and its `typescript`, `javascript`, `python`, `dockerfile`, `yaml` and `generic/secrets` directories were run instead. That is comparable to, but not verified as a superset of, what CI's advisory `sast.yml` runs (registry packs `p/typescript`, `p/javascript` and `p/react` at `--severity ERROR` only); the 143 security-relevant rows triaged here include many CI never surfaces.
- **The 2026-07-01 audit's remediation claims were spot-checked, not re-verified in full.** Where a lane re-found a July item it is marked `previously-reported`.
- **History is complete.** The clone was deepened before the finders ran until every commit reachable from `HEAD` was present (5,489 commits at the time; 6,889 across all fetched refs), so date and blame claims are not shallow-limited; `check:stale-docs` and `check:dead-code-candidate` ran on full history.
- **Stage 5 changed this document.** The adversarial review (§15) raised 37 corrections. Those against text the orchestrator wrote (§3, §4, §5, §8.3, §10, §11.1, §12, §14, §16) were applied in place; those against a finding's own text stand as `> **Correction (Stage 5, …)**` blockquotes under the finding, with the skeptic's grade left as it was; and the ten same-defect pairs the review found across lanes were merged, so §5 reports distinct defects. Where Stage 5 argues a grade up or down, both the grade and the argument are shown and the owner decides.

## 4. Machine evidence

Every gate below ran in this container on `d1e4ae7` before any finder started — offline, apart from four owner-approved read-only network operations: `git fetch --deepen` (GitHub), `npm audit` twice (the npm advisory endpoint), the Semgrep 1.168.0 install (PyPI) and the `semgrep-rules` clone (GitHub). Exit codes and decisive lines are copied from the logs; nothing is summarised from memory.

The dominant theme is different from 2026-07-01. Then it was the answer path dropping clinical numbers and caveats. Now the answer path looks unchanged since July as far as sampling shows (lane L02 confirmed the July fixes are still in place, but it sampled the three largest files and the live canary that pins retrieval behaviour did not run), and the three High findings sit in **clinical reference surfaces that the July audit did not have**: the medication record hero badge, the patient-medication interaction verdict, and the on-screen clinical table cleaner. The Medium tier is dominated by two things: **governance documents and gates that overstate what they prove** (hazard-controls register, privacy notice, PIA inventory, lexicon sign-off, migration-history gate, the "risk-routed PR mirror"), and **state that outlives the user** (a patient's physiology profile surviving sign-out, a favourites parser that rejects real timestamps, a tools card that leads to a 404). The sub-projects the owner asked about are, on the whole, closed properly: Ward Flow, Care Plan and the Caring Contacts mockup touch no data and are triple-gated; the two defects with real consequence there are the live launcher card and the missing P1 hazard log.

**Stage 5 adjustments (§15).** The review raised 37 corrections. Applied in place: the 11-entry merge above and every correction to orchestrator-written text. Left as blockquotes under the finding, with the skeptic's grade unchanged: the red team's arguments that H2, M8, M18 and M22 are a grade lower by the report's own §2 definitions, and the blue team's arguments that L30 (one public build flag disables the administrator gate in production), L6 (patient identifiers in session storage, once the production lock lifts) and the `#J43Z6B` tenancy corroboration deserve Medium. Those grades are the owner's call; the arguments sit beside the evidence.

## 6. High severity (3)

### H1 · Formulation badge drops the decimal point and misreads mg/mL, so the medication hero shows a wrong strength (varenicline 0.5 mg → "5 mg tablet")

`src/lib/medication-badges.ts:91 (formulationShortLabel: `cleaned.match(/(\d+)\s*mg/i)`); consumers src/components/clinical-dashboard/medication-record-page.tsx:278,318; src/components/clinical-dashboard/medication-prescribing-workspace.tsx:424; src/lib/cross-mode-links.ts:118` · correctness · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L03c (L03c-F1)

> **Correction (Stage 5, red team):** The cited consumer lines do not call formulationShortLabel (module-private, medication-badges.ts:84); they call medicationIdentityBadges/medicationAccessBadges, which reach it at :247 and :319. Substance unchanged: the committed snapshot reproduces varenicline/meloxicam/methadone → '5 mg tablet', nicotine-lozenge → '5 mg'.

- **Defect:** The regex captures only the integer run immediately before "mg", so a decimal strength is read from its fractional digits, and a concentration such as "5 mg/mL" is read as a 5 mg unit strength. The label is then rendered as an identity badge ("5 mg tablet") in the record hero, the prescribing result rows and cross-mode links.
- **Trigger:** Any snapshot record whose Route/formulation quick row or Oral routes form row states a decimal or per-mL strength before the first integer-mg strength. Present in the committed data today: varenicline "Tablets (0.5 mg, 1 mg)" → "5 mg tablet"; meloxicam "Tablets (7.5 mg, 15 mg)" → "5 mg tablet"; nicotine-lozenge "Lozenges (1.5 mg, 2 mg, 4 mg)" → "5 mg"; buprenorphine-naloxone "films (2/0.5 mg and 8/2 mg)" → "5 mg"; methadone "Oral liquid syrup (5 mg/mL). Tablets (10 mg)" → "5 mg tablet"; cetirizine/loratadine "Oral liquid (1 mg/mL)" cases similar.
- **Impact:** A clinician opening varenicline or meloxicam sees a strength badge for a tablet that does not exist (varenicline is 0.5 mg / 1 mg; meloxicam 7.5 mg / 15 mg) — a tenfold error on the record's most prominent line. The full text is still in the sections below, but the badge is the glanceable value the hero is designed around, and the same label appears on prescribing result rows.
- **Proof:** not executed in-app — traced, then reproduced offline: copied the function body verbatim into `node -e` over data/medications-snapshot.json; 181 quick/oral-routes values produce a label that is not the first strength in the text, 8 of them by dropping a decimal (list above) and ~40 by reading an mg/mL concentration as a tablet strength. tests/medication-badges.test.ts only asserts the integer case ("333 mg EC tablet", line 28) and passed (77/77 in the focused run). The repo already knows this class of bug: src/lib/medications.ts:224-226 documents `firstClinicalSentence` as fixed for "1.5 mg", but formulationShortLabel was not.
- **Fix sketch:** Match `(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(mg|mcg|µg|g)(?!\s*/)` (exclude per-mL/per-5mL concentrations, keep the unit that was matched), or emit no badge when the first strength token is a concentration or a combination ("2/0.5 mg"). Add unit cases for 0.5 mg, 7.5 mg, 5 mg/mL and 2/0.5 mg.
- **Caution:** Do not widen to a first-number fallback — the generator comment at scripts/build-medication-interaction-index.ts:119-124 explains why bare first-token heuristics misfire on this corpus.
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** Line reads exactly as claimed and the trigger is present in committed data; the trailing-parenthetical strip at :87 does not help because the strength parenthetical is followed by more text (varenicline) or a brand parenthetical (meloxicam). A tenfold-wrong strength on the hero identity badge is a number a clinician can act on, so High stands. Fix sketch (decimal-aware regex, exclude `/mL`) does not touch any AGENTS.md never-touch surface; the existing 333 mg test keeps passing.

### H2 · A documented LOW-severity interaction composes to the success verdict: green shield, headline "No alert found", with "1 interaction" underneath

`src/lib/medication-interactions.ts:163 (`low: "success"`, also none/safe/beneficial 164-166); src/components/clinical-dashboard/medication-considerations.tsx:140 (PATIENT_VERDICT_COPY.success = "No alert found"), :102-134 (verdictSummaryBadge keeps tone = verdict.tone when findings > 0), :153-180 (PatientVerdictSignal renders copy.label above summary.label)` · correctness · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L03c (L03c-F2)

> **Correction (Stage 5, red team):** By the report's own §2 definition this is Medium, not High: the verdict band prints '1 interaction' directly under the headline (medication-considerations.tsx:104-118, :178) and the detail panel lists the row (:477-485), so the answer is contradictory rather than caveat-less. Keep High only if §2 is widened to 'a contradictory verdict band'.

- **Defect:** SEVERITY_TONE maps the corpus token LOW to the same `success` tone used for "checked, nothing found". composeMedicationVerdict then returns tone `success` for a patient whose only finding is a LOW row, and PatientVerdictSignal prints the success copy ("NO ALERT FOUND / From entered details") directly above the summary label "1 interaction". In the detail panel the interaction itself renders inside a success-toned InlineNotice with the ShieldCheck icon (medication-considerations.tsx:479-485).
- **Trigger:** Patient medication list containing a counterparty of any of the 34 LOW / 9 NONE / 5 SAFE / 3 BENEFICIAL rows in data/medication-interaction-index.json. Concrete pairs today: cefepime + tramadol-ir ("additive neurotoxicity if given with other seizure-threshold lowering drugs"); mesalazine + digoxin ("Decreases absorption of Digoxin"); montelukast + phenytoin ("reduce montelukast levels"); lactulose or sodium-picosulfate + any of 34 antibiotics; acetylcysteine + 7 IV-line-incompatible agents.
- **Impact:** The module's own header (lines 15-35) names unearned reassurance as its worst failure, and the verdict band is the safety hierarchy for result rows (comment at lines 146-152). A clinician scanning green rows reads "No alert found" for a pair the catalogue explicitly documents as interacting; the contradiction is only visible in the smaller second line. The source wording is shown verbatim further down, so the information exists, but the composed verdict is false.
- **Proof:** not executed in the browser — traced: node -e over the index lists every green-tone row (49 rows; cefepime→tramadol-ir, mesalazine→digoxin shown above are `resolved: true` with counterparties). Code path: interactionFromRow → SEVERITY_TONE.low = success → highestTone success → composeMedicationVerdict candidates [success] → tone success (not incomplete) → verdictSummaryBadge label "1 interaction" with tone success → PatientVerdictSignal copy "No alert found". tests/medication-interactions.test.ts:158-159 pin only critical→danger and unknown→neutral; the 2026-08-12 commit d87f7b115 introduced the mapping without a test for the composed headline. git blame shows it has never been revisited.
- **Fix sketch:** Map low/none/safe/beneficial to `info` (or a new "documented, low" tone) so a found row never shares the all-clear tone; alternatively keep the tone but make PATIENT_VERDICT_COPY depend on interactionCount > 0 ("Low-severity interaction documented"). Add a test asserting a LOW-only result never yields the "No alert found" headline.
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** Chain traced end to end from index row to rendered headline; no guard found. The module header (:15-35) states green must be unreachable on incomplete analysis but does not address a documented low-severity finding sharing the all-clear tone. The headline "NO ALERT FOUND" over a catalogue-documented interaction is a caveat-less reassurance a clinician scanning result rows can act on; High retained even though the second line and detail panel show the row. Fix sketch (map low/none/safe/beneficial to `info`, or make copy depend on interactionCount) is outside RAG ranking surfaces; SEMANTIC_TONE_PRIORITY ordering must be checked so `info` never outranks danger/warning.

### H3 · clinicalOnly table cleaning drops header cells but not body cells, shifting clinical values under the wrong column heading

`src/components/AccessibleTable.tsx:130-132 (header .filter(Boolean) vs unfiltered body) and :172 (displayRows indexes body by header position)` · correctness · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L13 (L13-F1)

- **Defect:** clinicalOnlyTable() cleans every kept header with cleanClinicalTableText() and then drops headers that clean to empty (`.filter(Boolean)`), but applies no matching drop to body rows. AccessibleTableMarkup then builds each display row as `header.map((_, index) => row[index])`, so once one header is removed every later body cell is read one position to the left and the last cell is silently discarded.
- **Trigger:** Any answer or visual-evidence table (output-panel.tsx:193, visual-evidence.tsx:162 both pass `clinicalOnly`) whose header cell is a metadata word plus a number — e.g. `Source: 3`, `Page 2`, `Citation 1`, `Chunk 4`. isMetadataHeader() only matches the bare word (`^source$` etc.), so such a header survives column removal and is then blanked by metadataCellPattern.
- **Impact:** A clinician sees a dose/level/interval printed under the wrong heading (e.g. the 'Max dose' column shows the source name) and the real value vanishes from the rendered table. This is the on-screen clinical table, not an export, and the low-confidence caveat is not raised because normalisation already succeeded.
- **Proof:** node replica of the exact regexes and the line-130/132 logic on header ['Drug','Source: 3','Max dose'] with rows [['Lithium','RANZCP','1200 mg'],…] produced header ['Drug','Max dose'] and unchanged 3-cell rows; with line 172's `header.map((_, i) => row[i])` the 'Max dose' cell renders 'RANZCP' and '1200 mg' is dropped. Not executed in the React component — traced.
- **Fix sketch:** Compute the kept indexes after cleaning (drop an index when its cleaned header is empty) and apply the same index list to body rows; or keep an empty header as `Column N` instead of filtering. Add a DOM test with a 'Source: 3' header asserting cell/heading alignment.
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** Reproduced offline from the exact source regexes and lines; no unit test pins clinicalOnlyTable, and the normalizer upstream does not neutralise a 'Source: 3' / 'Page 2' / '<file>.pdf' header (only bare words are treated as metadata columns). Ledger grep for clinicalOnly/metadata column/GEN-H3 finds nothing. Runtime precondition is data-shaped (an answer or OCR table whose header cleans to empty) but plausible in both callers, and when it fires a signed-in clinician sees a dose under the wrong heading with no low-confidence caveat — the High criterion. Fix (compute kept indexes after cleaning and apply to body, or keep 'Column N') is local to the component and touches no RAG/ranking surface; the semgrep ReDoS hit at AccessibleTable.tsx:23 is a separate, unrelated pattern.

## 7. Medium severity (30)

### M1 · Copied ward note / clipboard silently truncates clinical tables to 6 rows with no marker, while the on-screen table can be expanded

`src/lib/ward-output.ts:625 (and :662)` · privacy/clinical · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L02 (L02-F2)

- **Defect:** clinicalTableToTextRows() emits `normalized.body.slice(0, 6)` and formatDisplayedVisualEvidenceForClipboard() emits `normalized.body.slice(0, 6)`; neither appends any 'N more rows — see source' line. The on-screen AccessibleTable (src/components/AccessibleTable.tsx:169) shows a preview with an expand control, so the clinician can see all rows on screen but the pasted artefact drops rows 7+ without saying so.
- **Trigger:** formatWardNote / formatAnswerForClipboard / formatDisplayedVisualEvidenceForClipboard on any answer whose threshold or dose table has more than six body rows (e.g. a clozapine ANC/WCC action grid, a titration schedule, a lithium level table).
- **Impact:** A titration or monitoring table pasted into a record is missing its later rows (often the highest-dose or most severe-threshold rows) and reads as complete. This is the same class of hazard the 2026-07-01 H4 fix addressed for the low-confidence caveat, now for completeness.
- **Proof:** Code trace (not executed): ward-output.ts:625 `const visibleBody = normalized.body.slice(0, 6);` followed at :627-636 by the caption, optional low-confidence caveat, header and `...visibleBody.map(...)` only; :662 `...normalized.body.slice(0, 6).map(...)`. grep for 'more rows|truncat|showing' in ward-output.ts returns only these slices. tests/ward-output.test.ts (35 tests, passed in this session) has no case with >6 rows.
- **Fix sketch:** When body.length > 6 append a line such as `(6 of N rows shown — open the source for the full table)` in both emitters, mirroring the existing quote-truncation warning at evidence-panels.tsx:1482; add a ward-output test with 8 rows asserting the marker.
- **Caution:** Clinical output surface; keep the caveat lines already pinned by tests (H4).
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** The on-screen table tells the clinician 'Showing 6 of N rows'; the pasted ward-note/clipboard artefact drops rows 7+ silently and reads as complete. Trace and proof both hold. Not High because no number is wrong and the artefact is user-directed output the clinician composes, but a titration/threshold table missing its highest rows in a record is a clinical completeness hazard. The suggested marker line does not touch any AGENTS.md never-touch surface; keep the H4 caveat line pinned by existing tests.

### M2 · Privacy Impact Assessment data inventory omits the browser-side answer-thread and recent-query persistence of raw clinical query text

`docs/privacy-impact-assessment.md:78 (data inventory table) vs src/lib/answer-thread-storage.ts:3-8,195-238` · privacy/clinical · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L02 (L02-F3)

- **Defect:** answer-thread-storage.ts writes the raw query text, the full generated answer and the source excerpts for up to 12 turns (≤4.5 MB, 12-hour TTL, key `clinical-kb-answer-thread:<ownerId>` including the `guest-tab-session` owner) to window.sessionStorage; recent-query-storage.ts stores the last 5 raw queries per owner. The PIA's only browser-storage statement is that Safety-Plan content stays 'in the current browser tab … no API request or browser-storage write' (lines 29, 78, 144) and the inventory table has no row for answer-thread/recent-query storage. grep of docs/privacy*.md and docs/clinical-governance.md for 'sessionStorage' returns nothing.
- **Trigger:** Any answer completed in the dashboard; the thread is restorable for 12 h on the same tab. Anonymous (public) users are persisted under the shared `guest-tab-session` key.
- **Impact:** The governance document the owner relies on for APP/PHI reasoning understates where incidental PHI (a typed patient scenario plus the derived answer) rests. Mitigations exist in code (sessionStorage dies with the tab, TTL, owner-scoped keys, clear-on-sign-out) but are undocumented, so a future change (e.g. localStorage) would not be caught as a PIA deviation.
- **Proof:** src/lib/answer-thread-storage.ts:6-8 (`answerThreadTtlMs = 12h`, comment 'bound how long raw query/answer text stays restorable'), :219-226 `window.sessionStorage.setItem(scopedStorageKey(ownerId), serialized)`; recent-query-storage.ts:27 setItem of raw queries. docs/privacy-impact-assessment.md grep for 'sessionStorage|session storage|answer thread' — no hits (traced, not executed beyond grep).
- **Fix sketch:** Add an inventory row: 'Answer thread + recent queries — browser sessionStorage (owner-scoped key, 12 h TTL, ≤12 turns, cleared on sign-out); contains raw query text, answer, source excerpts' and note the shared-workstation control; cross-reference tests that pin the TTL/clear behaviour.
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** The PIA's data inventory does not record that ordinary-search raw query text, the generated answer and source excerpts persist in browser sessionStorage for up to 12 h (owner-scoped, TTL-bounded, size-capped), nor the last-5 recent queries. Line 513 covers Clinical Ask only, so it does not neutralise the omission. This is documentation drift, but it concerns privacy of incidental PHI and the PIA is the governance document of record, so Medium stands per the re-grade rule. Fix is doc-only and breaks nothing.

### M3 · H2 residual: threshold sentences whose value is a bare integer are still dropped by the source-title heuristic

`src/lib/source-text-sanitizer.ts:54-72, 371-380` · privacy/clinical · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L02 (L02-F4) · previously-reported (2026-07-01 H2) — residual of the shipped fix

- **Defect:** The H2 fix rescues fragments that match clinicalThresholdSignalPattern (unit-bearing numbers, ranges, comparatives, decimals) but the comment at :67-68 records that 'Bare integers deliberately do NOT match'. sourceTitleFragmentPattern is still unanchored (`[^.;]{0,180}`), so a fragment such as 'A Glasgow Coma Scale score of 8 indicates severe head injury' is ≥38% 'title', has no concrete-action verb and no unit/range token, and is removed from clinicalProseUsefulness().text.
- **Trigger:** Source prose where a scale/protocol/guideline name precedes a bare-integer threshold with no unit or comparative (GCS 8, sedation scale 3, PANSS item scores, 'stage 2').
- **Impact:** The threshold sentence is silently absent from sanitized answer prose / safety chips (rag-answer-text.ts and ward-output consume `usefulness.text`), the exact failure class H2 was raised for, narrowed to bare integers.
- **Proof:** node -e replica of the three regexes copied from :54-71 — "A Glasgow Coma Scale score of 8 indicates severe head injury" → {mostlyTitle:true, action:false, threshold:false, dropped:true}; "The Sedation Scale score of 3 means unrousable to voice" → dropped:true; the unit/decimal variants ('ANC of 1.5', 'level of 1.2') are rescued. Function not executed directly; heuristics replicated exactly.
- **Fix sketch:** Extend the rescue to `(score|stage|grade|level|item)\s+(of\s+)?\d+` and to 'of N' after a scale name, or anchor sourceTitleFragmentPattern to stop at the first verb/number; pin with a GCS-8 test.
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** Reproduced: a sentence carrying a bare-integer clinical threshold after a scale/protocol name is blanked entirely by the title heuristic, exactly the H2 failure class narrowed to bare integers. The H2 comment records the bare-integer exclusion as a deliberate trade-off to keep dropping 'Appendix 1' noise, so this is a known-design residual rather than a regression, but the clinical consequence (threshold silently absent from sanitized prose/safety chips) is real. Medium: the value is omitted, not altered, and the citation link remains. A fix must keep 'Appendix 1'-style noise dropping and the R5 provenance-score hardening; it is answer-path, not ranking.

### M4 · Patient physiology profile survives sign-out, session expiry and account switch in the same tab

`src/lib/patient-profile-storage.ts:17,184-192 (write, no clear helper); src/lib/supabase/client.tsx:233-235, 264-268, 390-392, 400-402 (sign-out/expiry/user-change clear only recent queries, answer thread, signed-URL cache)` · privacy/clinical · **CONFIRMED** · safe_cleanup=false · lane L03a (L03a-F2)

Nothing removes that key except closing the tab: the sign-out, markSessionExpired and user-id-change handlers clear recent queries, the answer thread and the signed-URL cache but not the profile, and the settings dialog's privacy "Clear" only calls clearRecentQueries (settings-dialog.tsx:609,626).

- **Impact:** Clinician B's medication pages immediately evaluate renal/hepatic/QTc/pregnancy/interaction alerts against patient A's physiology and medication list, with the Patient details pill pre-filled — a wrong-patient decision-support context that looks intentional. The data is de-identified physiology, but it is patient-specific clinical context the app's own sign-out boundary comment (recent-query-storage.ts:78-80) says must not be inherited by the next person.

Not executed — traced.
- **Fix sketch:** Add `clearPatientProfile()` (removeItem + dispatch PATIENT_PROFILE_CHANGE_EVENT) in patient-profile-storage.ts and call it beside clearRecentQueries() in the sign-out, markSessionExpired and user-id-change paths of client.tsx; also include it in the settings privacy Clear. Optionally clear `clinical-kb-search-pins-v1` (search-pins.ts:31, localStorage, unscoped) at the same boundary — low sensitivity (pin names/destinations only).
- **Skeptic (CONFIRMED):** Every cited line confirms: the profile (age, renal/hepatic, QTc, pregnancy, allergies, medications) persists in sessionStorage across sign-out, expiry and account switch within the same tab, while the repo's own sign-out boundary clears every other clinical-context store. On a shared workstation the next clinician's prescribing alerts are evaluated against the previous patient's physiology. The pill is visibly pre-filled so the context is not caveat-less, and the data is de-identified physiology — Medium, not High. Fix sketch (clearPatientProfile called beside clearRecentQueries and in the settings Clear) is safe.
- **Merged at Stage 5:** M5 (L03b-F1, lane L03b) "Patient physiology profile survives sign-out and is not owner-scoped, so the next clinician on the same tab inherits the previous patient's prescribing context" — `src/lib/supabase/client.tsx:389-391; src/lib/patient-profile-storage.ts:17,152-167,184-192; src/components/clinical-dashboard/global-search-shell.tsx:1168-1171; src/components/clinical-dashboard/patie`; its skeptic (CONFIRMED): Traced end-to-end from the cited lines: sessionStorage is per-tab and survives auth changes; nothing subscribes the profile store to auth identity; readers (medication-considerations.tsx, patient-details-dock-action.tsx, medication-prescribing-workspace.tsx) compute alerts from whatever the tab holds. Medium stands: … Both lanes describe the same unscoped patient-profile persistence; L03b adds the owner-scoping angle (the next clinician on the same tab inherits the profile).
