## 14. Recommended fix order

Grouped the way the 2026-07-01 audit grouped them: what could mislead a clinician first, then privacy and access, then supply chain and gates, then governance-document truth, then hygiene. Each item names the smallest gate that would prove the fix.

**A. Clinical-answer and clinical-reference correctness (this week)**

1. **H1** decimal and mg/mL strengths in the medication badge — `test:focused -- --files tests/medication-badges.test.ts` with four new cases, then the medication record page in `npm run ensure`.
2. **H2** LOW-severity interaction shares the "No alert found" tone — `test:focused -- --files tests/medication-interactions.test.ts` asserting a LOW-only result never yields the success headline; check `SEMANTIC_TONE_PRIORITY` so the new tone never outranks danger.
3. **H3** `clinicalOnly` header drop misaligns body cells — a DOM test with a `Source: 3` header in `tests/accessible-table.dom.test.tsx`.
4. **M-class answer-path items in §6 that change rendered text** (safety-note regex `i` flag; clipboard six-row truncation marker; bare-integer threshold sentences; the `unknown` validation-status caveat) — each is answer-path, not ranking; PR line `RAG impact: no retrieval behaviour change — <reason>`, `npm run verify:pr-local` (not `verify:cheap`: for `source-text-sanitizer.ts` and `source-governance.ts` the scope classifier sets `rag_eval_changed`, which adds the offline RAG evals), and a read of `docs/rag-behaviour/safeguards.md` first.

**B. Wrong-patient and access boundaries (this week)**

5. **Patient physiology profile survives sign-out** (§6, L03a-F2 / L03b-F1) — clear it in `signOut`, on `markSessionExpired` and on user-id change, through the profile's own change event so cached subscribers refresh (`patient-profile-storage.ts:158-160` caches by raw string); say in the PR that clearing on `markSessionExpired` also wipes an in-progress context on a transient refresh failure; one case in `tests/patient-profile-storage.test.ts`; amend `docs/codebase-index.md:793`.
6. **Favourites snapshot rejects Postgres microsecond timestamps** (§6, L03a-F1) — widen the client regex, add a fixture with `.550361+00:00`, `test:focused`.
7. **Developer gate switched off by a public env flag in production** (L10-F1) — make `DeveloperAreaGate` honour `NEXT_PUBLIC_MOCKUPS_ENABLED` only under the proxy's double-flag exception (`proxy.ts:282`: `PLAYWRIGHT_OFFLINE_MODE` plus the mockups flag) — ignoring it outright in production breaks the isolated Playwright production build, whose `@mockup` journeys reach the gated prefixes only through that short-circuit — and assert the production case in `check:production-readiness`; `tests/proxy.test.ts` plus a gate unit test.
8. **Speech transcription route live without the Clinical Ask flag** (§6, L04-F1 / L12-F4) — check the flag before any Supabase or OpenAI call, return 404, set `Permissions-Policy: microphone=()` (and update the `microphone=(self)` pin in `tests/security-headers.test.ts:59` in the same change); `tests/speech-transcription-route.test.ts` with a flag stub. Land in the same PR the two other "dormancy is not real" items: the Clinical Ask catalogue fallback that substitutes the whole catalogue when nothing matches (L12-F1) and the stream route consuming the durable limiter before the flag check (L12-F2) — both must be fixed before `CLINICAL_ASK_ENABLED=true` is ever set, and activation is one env flag.
9. **Owner-scope gate stops at `src/app/api`** (§9, corroborates `#J43Z6B`) — extend `check-owner-scope-api.mjs` to `src/lib/**` files that import `createAdminClient`, with a complete allowlist for deliberate public-corpus reads (`owner_id IS NULL` by design) assembled first — `check:owner-scope` runs on every heavy PR, so a half-complete allowlist reddens all of them; `npm run check:owner-scope`.

**C. Supply chain and gates (next two weeks)**

10. **browserslist high in the production tree** (§6, L16-F1 / L17-F1) — `npm update browserslist` (registry call; owner approval), `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` green (registry-backed, approval), lockfile-changed CI job green — observable only once the PR is out of draft, because `safety` skips drafts.
11. **No vulnerability signal for the worker's Python stack** (L16-F2) — add a `pip` ecosystem to Dependabot for `worker/python` and `eval/docling`, and make the Trivy scan report somewhere a person looks.
12. **`verify:pr-local` heavy plan omits `check:owner-scope`, `check:function-grants`, `check:migration-role`** (L15-F2) — add a `staticHeavyGuards` list and change the `assertPlan` self-test expectations in the same commit (`check:verification-plan` runs on every PR with no condition, so a half-done edit fails all of them); or reword CLAUDE.md so nobody believes it mirrors CI. Stage 5 counts the gap at the three guards plus 14 other `static-pr` steps, not 12.
13. **`enrich:documents` writes across all owners and calls OpenAI by default** (L15-F1) — opt-in `--all-owners`, `--write` required, `checkSupabaseProject()` first.
14. **`npm run test` becomes a destructive Postgres run when `CARING_CONTACTS_DATABASE_URL` is set** (L14-F2) — loopback-host assertion in the helper and scrub the variable in `offlineTestEnvironment` unless an explicit opt-in is present — and honour that opt-in inside `run-vitest.mjs` itself, because it applies `offlineTestEnvironment` to whatever `run-db-tests.mjs` passes; otherwise `caring-contacts:db:test` and CI's `caring-contacts-db` job break.
15. **`check:medication-interactions` runs in no CI job** (L03c-F4); **`check:clinical-hazard-controls` runs in no CI job and needs full git history** (L21-F1) — wire both into `static-pr` under the same condition as their siblings, bumping the counts `check:gate-manifest` parses in the same change, and give the hazard gate a full-history check with a clear message rather than a place in `verify:cheap` (there it would go red on every shallow clone, as it did in this audit's first run).
16. **Orphaned tests**: restore the 320 px AccessibleTable journey (L11-F1) and add `ui-tools-show-all` to the Playwright matcher (L14-F1) — a restored mockup journey goes into both `testMatch` and `mockupSpecPattern` in `playwright.config.ts`, and `ui-tools-show-all` also needs `scripts/playwright-pr-shards.mjs` and its test; note the merge-commit blind spot in `docs/agents/test-deletion-guard.md`.
17. **`.claude/settings.json` blanket `git push` allow** (L20-F1) — enumerate the safe spellings instead.

**D. Governance-document truth (next two weeks)**

18. **The P1 hazard log and message-review pack do not exist in the repository** (§6, L07-F3 / L19-F2) — recover them from the 2026-08-19 session outputs or write them, commit under `docs/caring-contacts/` (commit the files before adding them to `docs/README.md`, or `docs:check-links` fails every docs PR), then `issues:update` `#1S81R8` and `#TDKW4W`.
19. **Caring Contacts card advertised as ready on the live launcher** (§6, L07-F1 / L23-F1) — hide or mark "demonstration only" in production, keeping the Playwright offline exception so the workspace journey keeps its entry point; a test tying the card to `isCaringContactsDemoEnabled()`.
20. **Privacy notice says "no shared corpus"** (L21-F2) and **the PIA omits browser-side answer and query persistence** (L02-F3) — copy changes to `privacy-page-content.tsx` and the PIA inventory; no test pins the sentence (Stage 5 disproved the finder's claim that `tests/privacy-ui.test.ts` does — it pins only the heading), so re-run `tests/privacy-page.dom.test.tsx`, which renders the content.
21. **Hazard-controls gate proves existence, not assertion** (L21-F1); **lexicon sign-off not bound to content** (L03c-F3); **`check:migration-history` exits 0 on merged-but-unapplied** (L06-F2) — each is a small script change plus a self-test; the migration-history one goes in its own reviewed PR, because the gate is listed never-touch in the gate-consolidation audit and a failure on `localOnly` races the push-triggered `live-drift` run.
22. **`set_document_corpus_access_mode('public')` leaves child owners set** (L06-F1) — migration inside an approved window, with a validation guard migration per the contract; or the retrieval-predicate route, which is a protected RAG change.

**E. Hygiene and memory (as convenient)**

23. Check `docs/outstanding-issues-inbox/` before every closure — `#B0530F` and `#27TWKM` already have pending `done` requests from PR #2527, and filing them again is the duplicate-request trap §9 warns about for `#9P4XAE`. Then close or update the remaining stale ledger rows in §9 through `issues:done` / `issues:update` ( `#A95DRY`, `#9P4XAE`, `#HDCF2B`, the four hub rows, `#100`, `#W98GR7`, `#J43Z6B` narrowing, `#QSHHGK` baseline).
24. File the suggested new rows in §16.
25. Land the remaining documentation corrections in §11 that were not safe one-liners (site-map generator string, `llms.txt` brand name and its pinned test, `AGENTS.md` five-sections claim, `docs/database-drift-detection.md`, `docs/scripts-index.md` coverage).
26. Tick or retire the nine Ward Flow plans; add the six bed-release reducer tests; pause the Playwright clock.
27. Triage the knip export lists with `check:dead-code-candidate`; decide the mockup redirect policy once; record a decision on the mockups bundle ceiling.

## 15. Stage 5 — adversarial review

One reviewer, three lenses in turn (see §3: this is one agent's three readings, not three independent reviewers). Each section is reproduced verbatim apart from path normalisation. Where a lens disproved or materially sharpened a claim, the correction also appears as a `> **Correction (Stage 5, <lens>):**` blockquote under the affected finding.

### Red team

Method: every `file:line` cited in H1–H3 and M1–M35 was opened on this branch (HEAD `3be7c01b9`, base `d1e4ae7`); four proofs were re-run (the H1 badge helper over `data/medications-snapshot.json`, the M9 and M10 regexes, `measuredRequestedPage` for M29); no file was changed.

**Citation accuracy.** Every quoted line reads as the report says, except:

- **H1** — `medication-record-page.tsx:278,318`, `medication-prescribing-workspace.tsx:424`, `cross-mode-links.ts:118` do not call `formulationShortLabel` (module-private, `medication-badges.ts:84`); they call `medicationIdentityBadges` / `medicationAccessBadges`, which reach it at `:247` and `:319`. Substance holds: the replica over committed data yields varenicline, meloxicam and methadone → "5 mg tablet", nicotine-lozenge → "5 mg".
- **M4/M5** — `docs/codebase-index.md:794` is `:793` on this branch; commit 2's enumeration edit shifted it.
- **M6 / §8.3** — `git rev-list --all --count` is 6,889; "5,489-commit history" (§3, §8.3) is HEAD's first-parent count. The M6 proof used `--all`, so the claim stands under the larger number. `docs/caring-contacts` has 24 entries, not 23.
- **M7** — "pinned by tests/privacy-ui.test.ts" is false. No test references "shared corpus" or "another signed-in user"; `tests/privacy-ui.test.ts:49,131` pin only the heading "Who can access your data". This also breaks §10.2's "three tests keep the suite green by asserting a recorded defect" (two do) and the "test pins the text" clause in §14 item 20.
- **M8** — `source-governance.ts:151` is `:157`; `clinical-review-queue.ts:86` is `:87`; `docs/clinical-governance.md:19` is `:15` (line 19 is blank). The trigger paragraph misleads: `therapy-source-governance.ts:28` sets `clinical_validation_status` explicitly, and `registry-corpus.ts:125` / `sources/catalogue-core.ts:119` pass one through, so none of the named writers produces the absent-field shape.
- **M12** — `publish_approved_documents` is at `schema.sql:8473`, not "~8380".
- **M14** — "3 tests": the `describe("the production lock")` block has four cases (`tests/caring-contacts-session.test.ts:85,89,97,109`); M13 says four.
- **M17** — "4/min anonymous … 20/min authenticated" is the wrong bucket. `speech_transcription` is 12/60 s for owners (`api-rate-limit.ts:75`) and 3/60 s anonymous (`:96`); 4/min is `clinical_ask`. M16's numbers are right.
- **M19** — `ci.yml:465-474` is `:468-477` (M26 cites `:466-477`).
- **M24** — the omission is understated. For `static_heavy` the `static-pr` job also runs `check:upload-limit-parity` (`ci.yml:226`), `check:gate-manifest` (`:250`) and every docs/ledger step (`docs:check-index/-inventory/-scripts/-links`, `check:branch-review-ledger`, `check:outstanding-issues`, `check:ledger-write-discipline`), which `verify-pr-local.mjs` adds only when `docs_changed`. The gap is the three guards plus 14 others, not 12.

**Findings I can argue down.**

1. **Dedup did not finish.** Five Medium entries are the same defect twice: M4/M5, M6/M27, M13/M14, M16/M17, M19/M26. At Low: L84/L107 (site-map robots), L86/L106 (llms.txt brand), L122/L130 (knip mockup ignore), L81/L94 (15 modes), L70/L78/L93 (Ward Flow catalogue claim). "Medium 35" is 30 distinct defects and "173 findings" is at most 162; the §5 category counts are inflated by the same pairs. §14 and §16 already treat each pair as one item, so the summary contradicts the body.
2. **H2 is graded against a definition it does not meet.** §2 defines High as "a wrong number or a caveat-less answer". The band prints "1 interaction" one line under the headline (`medication-considerations.tsx:104-118, :178`) and the detail panel lists the row with its verbatim note (`:477-485`); the module invariant (`medication-interactions.ts:15-35`) is about incomplete analysis, and LOW→`success` was a deliberate mapping in `d87f7b115`. The headline is contradictory, not caveat-less — Medium by the report's own rule unless the rule is widened to "a contradictory verdict band". The fix sketch is sound either way.
3. **M22 is contradicted by its own citation.** The status line it says "leads" (`docs/medication-interaction-lexicon-review.md:3`) continues: "That sign-off covers the mappings as they stood on that date only. Any lexicon change made since is NOT covered by it: re-check every term the change touches". The Outcome row is historically true. The document carries the caveat the finding says a reader lacks; the missing content hash is a P3 recommendation, not a Medium.
4. **M8 is a hypothesis without a subject.** Every writer inspected (`upload/route.ts:261`, `therapy-source-governance.ts:28`, `registry-corpus.ts:125`, `catalogue-core.ts:119`) sets a validation status; the report could not name a document in the `unknown` shape and says prevalence "may be zero". PLAUSIBLE with no established precondition is a Low until one provider-backed row proves otherwise.
5. **M13/M14 partly corroborate an open row.** `#4STSM1` (P2, open) already records that the synthetic production build is spec-approved and unimplemented; the 19 Aug listing (`layout.tsx:6-7`) and the 22–23 Aug lock are that row's two halves. The new evidence is only that the card is unconditional; the rest belongs in §9.
6. **M3 is a recorded design residual.** `source-text-sanitizer.ts:67-68` records the bare-integer exclusion as the trade-off that keeps "Appendix 1" noise dropping. That is an owner-visible decision in code; "H2 residual" implies the July fix was incomplete when it was scoped this way on purpose. Medium is defensible on consequence only.
7. **M12 asks for a fix the repository has already refused.** `tests/document-corpus-access-mode.test.ts:78` ("does not rewrite high-volume derived artifacts at the authorization boundary") pins the decision the `20260825025717` header explains (hundreds of thousands of rows, synchronous call, rollback surface). Option (a) reverses that by migration; option (b) is a protected RAG surface. Every live document has `owner_id NULL` (`#ZBAC9D`) and the 2026-07-05/06 migrations nulled the child rows, so the flip is a no-op on today's data. Honest disposition: a documented limitation of the switch plus a corrected header comment, not a P2 issue.
8. **M18 understates its own mitigations.** Without `--include-current` the script touches only documents `needsEnrichmentBackfill` (`:546-556`) says lack enrichment, at limit 25 and `CONCURRENCY = 1` (`:564`), and only with a populated `.env.local` holding a service-role key. Medium rests entirely on the sibling-script convention; that is a process Low unless the owner wants the convention enforced by a test.
9. **M34 and M35 are Medium by rule, not consequence.** M34's PIA sentence errs conservatively (describes a flow that cannot start); M35's subagents are advisory and carry Glob/Grep. Both are Low-shaped defects promoted by the privacy/safety-document clause.
10. **M19/M26 — the "lost weekly signal" is the signal.** A red scheduled run is exactly what `notify-ci-failure.yml:17` (CI is listed) reports to the owner; the audit is that alert arriving early.

**Cannot break** (one line each): H1 (reproduced on committed data); H3 (reproduced; `metadataHeaderPattern` `:13` is bare-word only and `metadataCellPattern` `:14-15` blanks "Source: 3"); M1; M2; M9 (reproduced); M10 (regex reproduced, live shape at `live-drift-forensics-2026-08.md:2369`); M11 (administrator-only, `:349`); M15 (`offlineTestEnvironment` is a blacklist, `test-environment.mjs:53-58`; `run-db-tests.mjs:30` tells the developer to `export` the variable); M20; M21; M23; M24; M25; M28; M29 (reproduced); M30; M31; M32; M33.

**Recommended fix order — what each item would break.**

- **A4** names `npm run verify:cheap`; `source-text-sanitizer.ts` and `source-governance.ts` classify `rag_eval_changed=true` (`ci-change-scope.mjs:333-338`), so the PR gate adds `eval:rag:offline` + `eval:rag:adversarial:offline`. Run `verify:pr-local`. `clinical-safety.ts` and `ward-output.ts` do not trip it.
- **B5** — clearing on `markSessionExpired` also wipes an in-progress prescribing context on a transient refresh failure (consistent with the answer thread, but say it). `getPatientProfileSnapshot` caches by raw string (`patient-profile-storage.ts:158-160`); a clear that does not dispatch `PATIENT_PROFILE_CHANGE_EVENT` leaves subscribers on the old profile.
- **B7** — making `DeveloperAreaGate` ignore the flag in production breaks the isolated Playwright production build: `chromium-mockups` reaches `/mockups/care-plan`, `/mockups/caring-contacts` and `/mockups/ward-flow` only because `mockupsEnabled()` short-circuits the gate (`developer-area-gate.tsx:19-21`), and `resolveDeveloperAccessState` has no Supabase there. The gate must adopt the proxy's double-flag exception (`proxy.ts:282`) verbatim or every `@mockup` journey under those prefixes fails.
- **B8** — `microphone=()` fails `tests/security-headers.test.ts:59`, which pins `microphone=(self)`.
- **B9** — `check:owner-scope` runs in `verify:cheap` and on every heavy PR in `static-pr` (`ci.yml:428-429`); a `src/lib` scanner without a complete allowlist for deliberate public-corpus reads (`owner_id IS NULL` by design) reddens every heavy PR.
- **C12** — a `staticHeavyGuards` list changes eight `assertPlan` expectations (`verify-pr-local.mjs:253-311`); `check:verification-plan` runs on every PR with no `if:` (`ci.yml:243`), so a half-done edit fails all PRs.
- **C14** — `run-vitest.mjs:59` applies `offlineTestEnvironment` to whatever `run-db-tests.mjs` passes, so scrubbing the URL breaks `caring-contacts:db:test` and the CI `caring-contacts-db` job (`ci.yml:1169`) unless the opt-in override is honoured inside `run-vitest`, not merely set by the caller.
- **C15, and the M30/M33 "add to verify:cheap:internal" sketches** — `check:gate-manifest` parses CLAUDE.md `(\d+) static/consistency gates` and `.claude/skills/gates/SKILL.md` `check N of (\d+)` (`check-gate-manifest.mjs:96-109`); adding a gate without bumping both counts (36→37, 39→40) fails `verify:cheap` and `static-pr`. Putting `check:clinical-hazard-controls` in `verify:cheap` also makes every shallow-clone container red (this audit's own first run) and makes `verify:cheap` red for everyone on 2026-11-23.
- **C16** — a restored mockup journey must be added to both `testMatch` and `mockupSpecPattern` in `playwright.config.ts` or it is orphaned exactly like M32; `ui-tools-show-all` also needs `scripts/playwright-pr-shards.mjs` and `tests/playwright-pr-shards.test.ts`.
- **D18** — adding the hazard-log path to `docs/README.md` before the file exists fails `docs:check-links` on every docs PR, not only this one.
- **D19** — the card gate must keep the Playwright offline exception or `ui-caring-contacts-workspace` loses its entry point.
- **D20** — no test pins the sentence (M7), so the prescribed `tests/privacy-ui.test.ts` edit is a no-op; `tests/privacy-page.dom.test.tsx` renders the content and is the one to re-run.
- **D21** — failing `check:migration-history` on `localOnly` races the ~34 s integration apply on the push-triggered `live-drift` run (`live-drift.yml:11-14`) and the gate is marked never-touch (`gate-consolidation-audit-2026-09-02.md:362`).
- **E23** — `#B0530F` and `#27TWKM` already have `done` requests in `docs/outstanding-issues-inbox/` (`62fb8f5c…`, `cd053a9a…`, from #2527); a second `issues:done` is the duplicate-request trap §9 warns about for `#9P4XAE`. Check the inbox before every closure in item 23.
- **C10** — "lockfile-changed CI job green" cannot be observed on a draft PR: `safety` skips drafts (`ci.yml:443`).

**Documentation corrections (§11.1).** None touches a gate-parsed string: `check:gate-manifest` reads only "static/consistency gates" (CLAUDE.md:98, unchanged) and the gates skill; `docs:check-index` and `docs:check-links` pass on the working tree (run here: "coverage OK: all 64 …" and "5433 repo path references resolve"). Two gaps: the table omits `docs/README.md` (+1 index line, present in the committed diff), and the Prettier evidence (`format:check`, 15:31) predates every edit in commit 2 and the report itself, so formatting of the changed files is unproven until `format:changed` runs. The split that leaves `hub-panels.ts:134`, `SPEC.md:78` and `DECISIONS.md:122` on "15" is deliberate and correctly declared.

### Blue team

**Under-graded.**

- **L30 / L10-F1.** One build-time flag opens all four administrator-gated subtrees in production: `developer-area-gate.tsx:19-21` returns children when `mockupsEnabled()` is true; `env.ts:452-459` has no production refinement; `instrumentation.ts:30-45` refuses `PLAYWRIGHT_OFFLINE_MODE` and `LOCAL_NO_AUTH` in production but not this flag; `scripts/production-readiness.ts` never mentions it. The report calls it "the single most consequential misconfiguration in the repository", schedules it for this week (§14 B7) and proposes P2 (§16) — and grades it Low. By §2 a defence-in-depth gap is Medium; composed with L31 (gate lives only in a layout) it is the developer hub's real boundary.
- **L6 / CRIT-F6.** Patient name and mobile drafted into `sessionStorage` (`plan-draft.ts:58`) and not cleared by sign-out. The only mitigation is the production 404 that open P2 `#4STSM1` exists to remove; when that lands this is M4/M5 with identifiers. Mark it "Medium on activation" now, and pair it with `#4STSM1`.
- **L16 / L12-F1.** If Clinical Ask were switched on, the Differentials and Specifiers tiers substitute the whole catalogue when nothing matches, so synthesis runs on unrelated evidence. Graded Low because dormant, and absent from §14 entirely; activation is one env flag. It belongs in the fix list as "must land before `CLINICAL_ASK_ENABLED=true`", beside L33 (stream route consumes the limiter before the flag), which is the same "dormancy is not real" class as M16/M17 and should ride the same PR.
- **§9 `#J43Z6B`.** Twenty-six service-role call sites in `src/lib` outside the only mechanical tenancy check, RLS a dead backstop repository-wide, five owner tables with no policy at all. It receives no finding; §9 marks the row "stale / re-check", which reads as reassurance. This is the exposure model for every clinician's uploads and deserves its own Medium with the 26 sites listed.
- **L4 / L08-F5.** Real WA crisis-line numbers printed on patient-facing paper with `verifiedOn: "2026-08-20"` (`care-plan/mockups/fixtures.ts:258,271,284`) and no re-verification cadence. A wrong crisis number is a harm path; "developer-gated" is the only reason it is Low, and it should say so.

**What the report declined to say.**

- "The answer path is comparatively well guarded" (§5) is inferred from the July fixes still being present, not from reading it: `rag.ts` (4,352 lines), `rag-extractive-answer.ts` (4,042) and `clinical-search.ts` (2,161) were sampled, and the one instrument that pins their behaviour — the 36/36 live canary — did not run. The supportable sentence is "unchanged since July as far as sampling shows".
- There is zero browser evidence. No Playwright journey ran (pinned Chromium absent). Two findings show journeys silently not running (M31, M32); nothing here shows the other 47 specs pass on `d1e4ae7`.
- Draft PRs skip `safety`, `coverage`, `build`, `ui-critical(-fast)`, `caring-contacts-db` and `db-reset-verify` (`ci.yml:443,505,585,653-656,701-706,1037,1138-1141`). §12's "draft with no auto-merge" is a safety statement that also means the audit's own PR will prove `static-pr` only until it is undrafted.
- §8.6 states that every mockup and gated-prototype chunk ships in the production bundle and then asserts "enumeration is impractical" without a build to test it against. Say it is asserted.
- Five Medium pairs and at least five Low pairs survived "dedup" — direct evidence that the single-skeptic-per-lane-group pass never cross-read lanes, which is the verification thinness §3 admits, realised.
- The repository has 292 npm scripts and 78 gates, and this audit needed a bespoke runner (`run-fast-gates.sh`) to execute 68 of them; no sentence tells the owner which five gates decide whether a change is safe. That is the finding behind every process Medium here and it is never stated.

**Residual risks that deserve a finding of their own** (each is one provider-backed read the owner can approve in a minute; list them as an approval menu, not as table cells):

1. L01 — anonymous rate-limit keying on the last `x-forwarded-for` entry (`public-api-access.ts:20-55`) with no documented Railway header contract; every anonymous ceiling in M16/M17 and on the answer route rests on it.
2. L21 — whether the four pg_cron retention jobs are live after `20260901033250`; the 30/90-day claims on `/privacy` and in the PIA rest on it, and M23 shows the drift gate cannot see them.
3. L16 — branch protection: whether `PR Policy` (the RAG-impact and deferred-deploy gates) is required or advisory; `docs/process-hardening.md:585` says only two checks should be required.
4. L10 — whether `NEXT_PUBLIC_MOCKUPS_ENABLED` is set on the live Railway `Database` service (see L30 above).
5. L15 — 53 scripts call `loadEnvConfig` and silently pick up `.env.local`; M18 is one instance of a class.

**Sub-project sections.**

- **8.1 Ward Flow** — "closed sandbox" understates the carrying cost: 13,364 lines, 441 unchecked plan boxes pinning 140 exports, 49 advisory-only journeys nobody runs, and three documents describing a pre-sandbox world. "Keep it exactly as it is" is a decision, not a finding. Justified on data: nothing there touches a provider or a store.
- **8.2 Care Plan** — gating is sound and traced. Under-graded on the one paper artefact (L4 above). Justified otherwise.
- **8.3 Caring Contacts live** — "RLS is real" is earned (213 tests on PG16 here, PG17 in CI). Unsaid: production without `CARING_CONTACTS_DATABASE_URL` gets an empty in-memory store (`store.ts:44-66`), so if the lock were ever opened without the URL, patient-shaped data would live in process memory with none of the audit-freeze or retention guarantees the section praises. State that those guarantees are Postgres-only. "Close the four stale ledger rows" double-counts: `#B0530F` and `#27TWKM` closures are already queued in the inbox.
- **8.4 Caring Contacts mockup** — accurate.
- **8.5 Developer hub** — "the one gated surface that reads real data" plus a one-flag bypass, a layout-only gate and an unlimited polled endpoint are four Lows that compose to a Medium; the section's recommendation order agrees, its grades do not.
- **8.6 Mockups** — 75 non-gated routes 404 at request time but ship in the bundle; the ceiling was re-baselined five times in 17 days (+129 %) with no recorded decision, graded Low; the design-system gate suite (29 s, three generated artefacts) is the heaviest static gate in the repository for a surface that never reaches production.
- **8.7 Clinical Ask** — recommendation is right; the fix list omits L16 and L33 (above).
- **8.8 Therapy Compass** — "in good order" accepts a badge as the control for 205 unsigned live clinical records; the sign-off backlog is the risk and it has no owner date.

**Lanes that sampled rather than read** (from §10's own column): L02 (RAG core), L03b (~15 of 133 dashboard files), L06 (~200 of 224 migrations pattern-scanned), L07 (`postgres-repository.ts` 2,500 lines sampled; 40 components unread), L11 (74 mockup components import-direction only), L14 (~980 of 1,032 test files grep-only), L15 (~25 of 287 scripts), L17 (lockfile integrity and resolved URLs not audited), L18 (116 root docs grepped), L19 (459 archived rows grepped), L20 (vendored skills sampled), L21 (PIA by section). Half the lanes, including the two highest-consequence trees. "Every tracked path had exactly one owner" is true and is not the same as "was read".

**Where the caution is justified** (one line each): not filing ledger rows — the serial-only discipline is mechanically enforced; not editing the five gate-parsed `AGENTS.md` sections; treating the knip lists as triage, never deletion (PR #2204); refusing every provider-backed read; leaving Ward Flow, Care Plan and the mockup twin untouched; deferring the PIA re-encoding (CRIT-F4) to its own reviewed change; not hand-fixing `hub-panels.ts:134` inside a documentation-only commit.

### Verification-router

Persona: pick the smallest correct gate, prove it with a decisive line, and hold the provider boundary. Sources checked: `package.json` scripts, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `scripts/verify-pr-local.mjs`, `scripts/ci-change-scope.mjs`, `scripts/check-gate-manifest.mjs`, and every log under `scratchpad/evidence/`.

**Claims about what ran.**

- §4 "Every gate below ran offline" is false for four rows: `git fetch --deepen=3000` (GitHub), `npm audit` twice (npm registry advisory endpoint), the Semgrep 1.168.0 install (PyPI, `semgrep-install.log`) and the `semgrep-rules` clone (GitHub, `semgrep-rules-clone.log`). All read-only and approved in Phase 0; write "offline or approved read-only network". The report's boundary is applied unevenly — M26 calls `npm update browserslist` "provider-backed, needs approval" while §4 files `npm audit` under offline; both talk to the same registry.
- Machine evidence rows against their logs: `lint` and `typecheck` lines are verbatim (`heavy-gates.log:3,6`) and 93 s / 79 s match the timestamps; `test` is verbatim (`:16-18`) but "`VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=3`" appears in no log and `run-vitest.mjs:58` caps shared-mode workers at 2 — drop the number or evidence it; `caring-contacts:db:test` is verbatim (`caring-contacts-db.log:9-10`) and took 16 s by its own timestamps, not 15; the `npm audit` cell elides `{"info":0,"low":0,"moderate":0,"high":1,"critical":0,"total":1}` (`npm-audit.log:4-5`) — quote it whole; the Semgrep line "Ran 925 rules on 1757 files: 3878 findings." is **not** in `evidence/semgrep-scan.log` — it is `scratchpad/semgrep/scan.stderr:34`, and `semgrep-scan.log:3` reads "results: 3878 errors: 30", thirty errors the report never mentions; `git deepen` is verbatim; `check:stale-docs` and `check:dead-code-candidate` are verbatim in `gate-stale-docs-and-dead-code.log` (the row does not name the log); `docs-links --all` is verbatim in the TSV.
- §4.2: 68 rows confirmed (66 gates + 2 advisory); "36 static / 39 total" matches `package.json` and CLAUDE.md:98; "the four below … plus one that passed on re-run" describes four rows — three environment facts plus the re-run. The hazard-controls first-run log's decisive lines are "reviewedCommit does not exist 883f1007a…", the shallow-clone signature the report diagnoses correctly.
- §10 rows L15 and L20 say Semgrep "failed before producing results … no results.json exists". Stale: `the Semgrep results file (session evidence; the first scan attempt produced none because the proxy blocked semgrep.dev)` (3.9 MB, 15:49) and `triage.tsv` (143 rows) exist and the scan ended 15:52, after those lanes ran. §3/§4 are right; §10 must say the lane text predates the scan.
- §3 "check:drift … the replay is CI-only via the `Migration replay` job": that job (`db-reset-verify`) runs `supabase migration up --local`; `check:drift` runs only in `live-drift.yml` after merge (AGENTS.md:195). Two different gates.
- §3 files `check:production-readiness` as "provider-backed and approval-gated"; `scripts/production-readiness.ts` has an offline fail-closed path (`--ci`, `providerFreeCodexCloud`, `:13-25,187`) and a `check:production-readiness:ci` script — neither ran. Say "not run; the offline-tolerant `:ci` variant was also not run".
- §3 "a superset of what CI's advisory `sast.yml` runs": `sast.yml:46-51` uses registry packs `p/typescript`, `p/javascript`, `p/react` at `--severity ERROR`; a run over repository directories is not provably a superset of registry packs. Say "comparable, not verified as a superset".

**CI-routing claims** — verified against `ci.yml`: M30 (no `check:medication-interactions` anywhere), M33 (no `check:clinical-hazard-controls`), M19/M26 (audit step `:468-477`, `lockfile_changed` only), M25 (workflow names), M15 (`:1169`), M24 (`static-pr` list at `:337-437`, understated — see red team), §8.1 (`@mockup` journeys route to `advisory_ui_changed`, `ci-change-scope.mjs:112-116`), §8.3 (`caring-contacts-db` in `pr-required` needs `:1174-1186`; it runs only on `db_changed || static_heavy_changed` and never on a draft).

**The audit PR's own gate.** The branch diff is 16 files (1,722 insertions) plus two uncommitted files: this report and `data/repo-awareness-snapshot.json`. `node scripts/ci-change-scope.mjs --json --files <all 17>` returns `docs_changed=true`, `static_heavy_changed=true`, `build_changed=true`, `ui_changed=true`, `coverage_changed=true`, `docs_only=false`. The trigger is `data/repo-awareness-snapshot.json` alone: `data/**` is a route-payload input (`ci-change-scope.mjs:235-238`) carved out of `perf_changed` only (`:305`); CLAUDE.md alone classifies `docs_only=true`. The snapshot cannot be left out — `check:repo-awareness-snapshot` compares 583 documents (the audit file added one) against the committed 582. So this is **not** a docs-only PR and the light docs plan does not apply.

- **Local gate:** `npm run verify:pr-local`, 20 steps: `check:runtime`, `check:installed-lock-parity`, `format:changed`, `check:diff-integrity`, `sitemap:check`, `check:repo-awareness-snapshot`, `docs:check-index`, `docs:check-inventory`, `docs:check-scripts`, `docs:check-links`, `check:branch-review-ledger`, `check:outstanding-issues`, `check:ledger-write-discipline`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `test`, `build`, `check:rag:fixtures`, `check:medication-interactions`, `check:medication-lexicon-report`. `build` refuses while a dev server runs (exit 76) and needs the 8 GB heap; everything is offline. Decisive proof: the closing "PR-local verification summary: … failed: (none) … not reached: (none)" block plus "Test Files N passed (N)". Not stacked: `verify:cheap` (subsumed), `verify:ui` (`ui_changed` is the `data/**` side effect; no UI file changed), `eval:rag:offline` (`rag_eval_changed=false`). Commit the two uncommitted files first — `check:ledger-write-discipline` and `check:diff-integrity` range `d1e4ae7..HEAD` and were last run when that range was empty. Re-run here on the working tree: `check:repo-awareness-snapshot` in step (583), `check:diff-integrity` PASS, `docs:check-links` 5433 resolve, `docs:check-index` OK.
- **CI on the draft PR:** `changes` → `static-pr` with the heavy steps (`check:knip`, `check:maintainability-budgets`, `format:changed`, type/icon/brand/assets, `sitemap:check`, `check:repo-awareness-snapshot`, therapy/cross-mode/mha/forms-pdf drift, `check:design-system-contract`, `check:migration-role`, `check:function-grants`, `check:owner-scope`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `check:upload-limit-parity`, `check:medication-lexicon-report`, `check:gate-manifest`, the docs/ledger steps, `check:diff-integrity`) → `pr-required`; plus the `PR Policy` and `PR mergeability` workflows. Skipped while draft: `safety`, `coverage`, `build`, `ui-critical-fast`, `ui-critical`, `caring-contacts-db` (`draft != true` guards); `lighthouse-budget` (`perf_changed=false`), `db-reset-verify` (`db_changed=false`), `container-images`, `ingestion-sast`. Undrafting fires `ready_for_review` and runs `safety`, `coverage`, `build`, `ui-critical-fast`, `ui-critical` and `caring-contacts-db`.
- **What the report says:** nothing — §12 says only "a draft with no auto-merge". The status header should state the gate, the classification, and that the draft proves `static-pr` only.
- **PR body:** no RAG-protected path is touched, so no `RAG impact:` line is required; no migration is touched, so the deferred-deploy phrase gate is out of scope.
- **Formatting:** the `format:check` pass (15:31) predates commit 2 and this report; `format:changed` in the plan and the pre-push guard will check the pushed commit, not the working tree.

**Provider-backed or approval-gated commands the report implies were run:** none. The network operations that ran are the four above. Focused Vitest runs cited in H1, M13, M14 and M21 are offline. The PostgreSQL 16 behind `caring-contacts:db:test` was a local container process, not the Supabase project. Fix-order routing note: A4's `verify:cheap` is the wrong gate for `source-text-sanitizer.ts` and `source-governance.ts` (`rag_eval_changed=true` → `verify:pr-local` adds the offline RAG evals); C10's `npm update browserslist` and the follow-up `npm audit` are registry-backed and need approval, and their CI proof cannot be observed on a draft.

## Appendix C — Low severity detail (129)

**L1 · Logger key-redaction regex misses camelCase/snake_case query and answer keys** — `src/lib/logger.ts:13-14` · privacy/clinical · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L01-F4

SENSITIVE_KEY uses `\bquery\b` and `\banswer\b`; `_` and letters are word characters, so keys `queryText`, `query_text`, `rawQuery`, `normalizedQuery`, `question`, `answerText`, `answer_text` are NOT redacted while bare `query` is. The header comment promises that clinical query/answer content never reaches the log. **Impact:** A single careless log call would write raw clinical query text (possible patient identifiers) to Railway logs, which the redaction layer is meant to prevent regardless of call-site discipline. **Fix:** Replace `\bquery\b` / `\banswer\b` with unanchored `query|answer|question` (accepting over-redaction of e.g. `queryClass`), or match on token boundaries that treat `_` and case changes as separators; add the camel/snake cases to tests/logger.test.ts. **Skeptic:** Reproduced offline with the exact regex. No call site passes such a key today and the Sentry bridge has its own allowlist, so nothing leaks now; the defect is that the redaction layer promises call-site-independent protection it does not deliver for the most natural key spellings. Low as defence-in-depth. Fix sketch (unanchored …

**L2 · Favourites pins and last-opened timestamps are stored in unscoped localStorage and survive sign-out, unlike recent queries** — `src/components/favourites/favourites-storage.ts:3-4,68-70,98-101,114-116,142-145; src/lib/supabase/client.tsx:389-391` · privacy/clinical · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L03b-F5

`database:favourites:last-opened-v1` and `database:favourites:pinned-v1` are global localStorage keys (90-day TTL) read by favourites-command-library-page.tsx:1335,1454. Sign-out clears recent queries, the answer thread and the signed-URL cache, but not these keys, and the keys carry no owner id. **Impact:** Leaks which guideline/registry items another clinician opened recently (item ids and times only — no patient data), and orders the Favourites library by someone else's usage. Same class the 2026-07-13 audit closed for recent queries, at lower sensitivity. **Fix:** Namespace both keys by owner id (mirroring the owner-scoped recent-queries store) or clear them in `signOut`; add a case to the favourites storage tests. **Skeptic:** Traced. Payload is item ids and timestamps only (DEFAULT_PINNED_ITEM_IDS are seed slugs), so Low is right. Same pattern the recent-queries fix (global-search-shell.tsx:617-619) addressed. Fix (owner-namespaced keys or clear in signOut) touches nothing load-bearing.

**L3 · Assertion-tagging warning path can echo up to 120 characters of chunk text through console.warn, bypassing the worker's safe-logging boundary** — `worker/python/analyze_assertions.py:99; worker/assertion-tagging.ts:149-151` · privacy/clinical · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L05-F7

`warnings.append(f"skipped malformed chunk: {chunk!r:.120}")` serialises the chunk object (which carries the clinical `text`) into the warning list, and `annotateChunkAssertions` prints every warning verbatim with `console.warn`. All other worker logging goes through `safeErrorLogDetails`/`safeIngestionJobLog`. **Impact:** Guideline text could reach container logs/Railway log drains, contrary to the documented privacy posture; no patient data is involved (guideline corpus). **Fix:** Log the chunk index or id only (`f"skipped malformed chunk at index {i}"`). **Skeptic:** Code path exists exactly as described and bypasses the worker's safe-logging convention, but it is unreachable from the production worker as wired (ids and string text are constructed by main.ts). Content at risk is guideline text from a corpus the ledger records as fully public (2,851/2,851 public_corpus=true), not patient data. Low; trivial fix …

**L4 · Real WA crisis-line numbers print on patient-facing paper with no re-verification cadence** — `src/components/care-plan/mockups/fixtures.ts:200,217,235 (CMHT afterHoursTelephoneDisplay) and :249-300 (publicCrisisContacts, verifiedOn '2026-08-20')` · privacy/clinical · PLAUSIBLE · safe_cleanup=false · L08-F5

MHERL Perth 1300 555 788, MHERL Peel 1800 676 822 and Rurallink 1800 552 002 are the only non-fictional data in the prototype, deliberately (sdd-ledger.md:625-632 records that printing a fictional number under a real service name was rejected). They carry `verifiedOn: '2026-08-20'` and a comment 'If a number … has changed since, correct it here', but nothing — no test, gate, or outstanding-issues row — ages that date, while the same file models CMHT contacts as 'review_due' after months. These numbers render on the printed Personal Safety Plan and Patient Plan a person is meant to take home. **Impact:** A patient-facing printed sheet could carry a dead or redirected crisis number; this is the one place the 'everything is synthetic' framing does not protect the reader. **Fix:** Add a unit assertion that every publicCrisisContacts.verifiedOn is < 12 months old (fails loudly on 2027-08-20), and an outstanding-issues row naming the owner who re-verifies against the cited WA Health pages. **Skeptic:** Factually right: four real public numbers are pinned by test but nothing ages the 2026-08-20 verification date. The impact is overstated — the prototype is administrator-gated synthetic data behind the production /mockups block, so no patient takes this sheet home today; the risk only materialises if the fixture is copied into a production screen. …

**L5 · public/brand/preview.html preconnects to Google Fonts from the clinical origin and is not noindexed** — `public/brand/preview.html:2-7 (mirrors docs/brand/preview.html; byte-identity pinned by tests/developer-hub-panels.test.ts:186-191)` · privacy/clinical · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L13-F6

The served brand sheet carries `<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com">`, a gstatic preconnect, and a Google Fonts stylesheet. The proxy CSP (`style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`; font-src falls to default-src 'self') blocks the stylesheet, so the page renders in fallback fonts, but `rel=preconnect` is not CSP-governed and still opens a connection (visitor IP) to Google. Unlike /mockups/:path*, /brand/* gets no X-Robots-Tag. **Impact:** Third-party contact from a clinical origin that the privacy page does not list, plus an indexable page that half-renders; both are small but contradict the 'no external requests' discipline offline.html and the PWA docs apply to public assets. **Fix:** Inline the two faces (or use the vendored Geist) in the generated sheet, regenerate both copies, and add `/brand/:path*` to the noindex header block. **Skeptic:** Preconnect is not CSP-governed, so the visitor's browser opens a TCP/TLS connection to Google on load of a page linked from the signed-in Developer Hub; the stylesheet itself is blocked, so the page renders in fallback fonts — both halves of the claim hold. Low: the leak is an IP-level connection from an administrator-facing page with no clinical …

**L6 · Caring Contacts plan draft (patient name and mobile) in sessionStorage is not cleared by app sign-out, session expiry or account switch** — `src/components/caring-contacts/workspace/plan-wizard/plan-draft.ts:20-28 (three documented clear paths), :58 (PLAN_DRAFT_STORAGE_KEY), :514 (readPlanDraft by re` · privacy/clinical · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · CRIT-F6

> **Correction (Stage 5, blue team):** Mark 'Medium on activation': the only mitigation is the production 404 that open P2 #4STSM1 exists to remove; when the synthetic production build lands this becomes the M4/M5 class with a patient name and mobile.

plan-draft.ts deliberately chose sessionStorage so a draft dies with the tab and enumerates 'THREE WAYS THE DRAFT GOES AWAY' (tab close, activation, discard). Sign-out is not one of them: the app's signOut, markSessionExpired and user-id-change handlers clear three named stores and never touch `caring-contacts:plan-draft`, and the workspace has no sign-out of its own (rg -i 'sign.?out' src/components/caring-contacts/workspace src/app/caring-contacts → none). A clinician who signs out mid-draft and hands the same tab to a colleague who signs in and opens the same referral gets the previous … **Impact:** Contradicts the module's own stated ruling ('leaves a patient's details … for the next person at that machine, which is the failure the whole ruling exists to prevent'). Mitigated today: the workspace is a 404 in every production process (L07-F1) and sessionStorage is per-tab. **Fix:** Export a `clearCaringContactsBrowserState()` from plan-draft.ts and call it from the three account-transition handlers in client.tsx alongside the existing clears (or register clinical browser stores centrally so the sign-out path enumerates them all — same fix the patient-profile findings need). **Skeptic:** Chain traced end-to-end: the draft lives under one sessionStorage key, the only clear calls are the wizard's own (activation, discard, referral change), and none of the three account-transition handlers in client.tsx touch it; sessionStorage survives a sign-out and a subsequent sign-in in the same tab, so the trigger (A signs out mid-draft, B …

**L7 · Every active calculator cites an internal governance spec as a clinical 'Source', rendered as a link into the GitHub repository** — `data/calculators/evidence.json (source:governance, url https://github.com/BigSimmo/Database/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-calculators-clinical-saf` · privacy/clinical · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · CRIT-F8

The calculator evidence registry has nine sources; eight are real instrument references (PHQ-9 Kroenke 2001, GAD-7 Spitzer 2006, K10 ABS, CAGE, AUDIT-C, MDQ, SAD PERSONS, Y-BOCS). The ninth, 'Calculator clinical-safety governance record' (type internal_governance_record), is attached to every fixture's sourceIds and rendered on the public /calculators detail page in the same 'Sources:' line, as a hyperlink to a repository blob URL. A clinician sees a repository spec listed as evidence beside a validation study; the page also advertises the source repository path from a clinical UI. **Impact:** Citation quality on a clinical surface the repo says must be 'verifiable against linked sources': the governance record is provenance, not evidence, and its link resolves to a Markdown spec rather than a clinical source. No safety effect on scoring; quarantine statuses were verified consistent … **Fix:** Render internal_governance_record sources as an unlinked provenance note ('Reviewed under the calculator clinical-safety governance record, 2026-09-01') or drop them from the user-facing Sources line while keeping them in claim provenance; add evidence.json to the L03c-style clinical data assets … **Skeptic:** Traced from data to render: the governance record is attached to every active calculator's sourceIds and rendered identically to a validation study — as a hyperlink to a GitHub blob URL — on the production /calculators/search detail surface. No guard distinguishes source type, and no test or ledger row covers it. Severity Low: the scoring and …

**L8 · Sentry ignoreErrors uses substring matchers (/404/, /Not Found/i, "NotFound") that can silently drop genuine server errors** — `src/sentry.server.config.ts:24-35 and :100` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L01-F5

Sentry applies `ignoreErrors` as substring/regex matches on the error message and type before `beforeSend`. `/404/` drops any exception whose message merely contains '404' (e.g. a Supabase Storage 404 during upload cleanup, or an OpenAI 404 for a retired model), and the bare string "NotFound" matches any custom error type containing it. docs/error-tracking.md does not mention that these classes are discarded. **Impact:** The failure produces no Sentry event; operators see only Railway logs. No custom _NotFound_ error class exists in src today, so current impact is limited to message-based 404s. **Fix:** Anchor the patterns to the intended Next.js not-found shapes (e.g. /^NEXT_NOT_FOUND$/ or the exact 'Request failed with status code 404' text) and document the ignore list in docs/error-tracking.md. **Skeptic:** Lines quoted match. Sentry's ignoreErrors matches strings as substrings and regexes against message/type before beforeSend, so `/404/` discards any provider error whose message carries 404 (OpenAI model-not-found, Storage object-not-found). Observability gap only, no clinical or data impact, undocumented: Low. Anchoring the patterns and …

**L9 · Legacy `/?mode=<differentials|specifiers|favourites>&q&run=1` links drop queryMode and scope filters one hop before the redirect that promises to keep them** — `src/lib/legacy-home-redirect.ts:29-38 (destination.search = ""; only q/focus/run re-added) vs src/lib/consolidated-mode-home-redirect.ts:79-82 ('Every other que` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L03a-F5

For the three modes in legacyModePaths, the proxy's first redirect rebuilds the URL from scratch, keeping only q, focus and run. The consolidated redirect that runs next (for differentials/specifiers) preserves scope._, queryMode and scopeRef — but they are already gone. The shared-home page's own submitted-link path (src/app/(search-app)/page.tsx:80-90) would have preserved them via readSearchNavigationContext, but the proxy pre-empts it for GET/HEAD. **Impact:** The results page renders unscoped/auto-mode results for a link the sender scoped; no error is shown, so the clinician may not notice the narrower context was lost. **Fix:** In legacyHomeRedirectUrl copy the incoming searchParams, delete only `mode`, and set q/focus/run — or route the three modes through consolidatedModeHomeTarget-style param cloning. **Skeptic:** Chain holds: for the three legacy modes the proxy strips scope._/queryMode/scopeRef one hop before the redirect that preserves them, and the page-level path that would have kept them never runs for GET. Only old `/?mode=...&run=1` links are affected (the app no longer emits run=1 there) and the result is a silently broader search, not a wrong …

**L10 · K10 live score can display a partial sum below the instrument's minimum possible score while incomplete** — `src/components/calculators/search-detail.tsx:456-459; src/components/calculators/calculator-ui.tsx:115; src/components/calculators/calculator-fixtures.ts:328-33` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L03b-F8

K10 items score 1–5 (minScore 10), but `deriveCalculator` sums only answered items, and the Score panel shows `derived.score / calc.maxScore` as soon as `started` is true. After three items a clinician sees e.g. '3 / 50', a value that cannot exist for the K10, next to the 'Incomplete' pill and '3 of 10 answered' progress line. **Impact:** The caveats are present (band and considerations are withheld until complete, copy is disabled, the progress label is shown), so no interpretation is wrong; but the running number is not a K10 score and could be misread at a glance. PHQ-9/GAD-7/CAGE/AUDIT-C are unaffected because their floor is 0. **Fix:** For instruments with minScore > 0, show the progress label instead of the partial numerator until complete (or render `MissingValue reason="not_yet_calculated"` until every item is answered), and add a jsdom assertion for K10. **Skeptic:** Traced to a live production route; a partial K10 sum below 10 is rendered next to the 'Incomplete' pill and progress label. Caveats are present, so no wrong interpretation — Low. Fix (withhold the numerator for minScore>0 until complete) does not touch RAG or ledger surfaces; a DOM assertion for K10 would be additive under diff-integrity.

**L11 · Committed-generation predicate fails open in TypeScript but closed in SQL when a document has no committed generation** — `src/lib/reindex-pipeline.ts:50-55; supabase/schema.sql:2570-2581 (is_committed_document_generation(uuid,uuid)); src/lib/document-detail.ts:226-234` · correctness · PLAUSIBLE · safe_cleanup=false · L04-F5

`isCommittedGenerationMetadata` returns true whenever `committedGeneration` is null, so a document whose `metadata.index_generation_id` is absent shows rows from ANY generation; `committedGenerationFilter` likewise returns null (no SQL filter). The SQL predicate used by `search_document_chunks` evaluates `row_generation = NULL` → NULL → excluded, hiding the same staged rows. During the first atomic reindex of a legacy (never-stamped) indexed document, the viewer (`loadAuthorizedDocumentDetail`) can therefore interleave staged, uncommitted chunks/pages/images/table facts with the live ones … **Impact:** Duplicate or half-built evidence (two copies of a dosing table, a staged table fact without its committed image) visible in the document viewer mid-reindex; cited page text may differ from what search returns. **Fix:** Make the TS predicate mirror SQL (a row that carries a generation is committed only if it equals the document's committed generation; absent document generation → only null-generation rows), and add a unit case for `committedGeneration: null, rowGeneration: 'x'`. **Skeptic:** The divergence is real and quoted, and the mid-reindex duplicate-row exposure follows from it for a document with no committed generation. But the TS behaviour is deliberately pinned by a unit test and documented, so the finder's fix_sketch would break tests/reindex-pipeline.test.ts:75-81 and must be argued as a behaviour change, not a bug fix. …

**L12 · One vision-rejected embedded image fails the whole document: Python path emits uncapped raw images with PDF-filter MIME types and the worker has no per-image error isolation** — `worker/python/extract_pdf_assets.py:1108-1122; worker/main.ts:1097-1107; src/lib/openai.ts:1149-1151; src/lib/ingestion.ts:3-15` · correctness · PLAUSIBLE · safe_cleanup=false · L05-F1

For every embedded image the Python extractor writes the raw stream bytes as-is (`document.extract_image(xref)`, line 1108) with no per-image byte or pixel ceiling (only the aggregate `maxArtifactBytes`/`maxArtifacts` budget applies; the render-pixel cap governs page renders, not embedded streams) and derives the MIME type from PyMuPDF's filter extension: `mime = "image/jpeg" if ext in ("jpg","jpeg") else f"image/{ext}"` (line 1122), which yields `image/jpx`, `image/jb2`, `image/tiff`, `image/bmp`, `image/pnm` for JPEG2000/JBIG2/other filters. The worker forwards `image.mimeType` straight … **Impact:** The whole document fails ingestion (or, in the atomic-reindex path, keeps its prior generation) because of one un-captionable image, with an OpenAI error string as the job's error message. The clinician sees a failed upload with no indication that the fix is 'skip that image'. No clinical text is … **Fix:** In extract_pdf_assets.py map non-web formats to PNG (re-encode via `fitz.Pixmap(document, xref).tobytes("png")` under the existing pixel budget) and cap a single embedded artifact's bytes; in main.ts wrap the per-task vision call so a non-retryable provider rejection records a `skip_reason` (e.g. … **Skeptic:** Every code link is quoted and holds. Two runtime preconditions could not be established offline: that PyMuPDF actually returns ext 'jpx'/'jb2' for those filters (documented upstream behaviour, not proven here) and that OpenAI rejects `image/jpx` data URLs with a non-retryable 400. Hence PLAUSIBLE. Downgraded to Low: #282 measured the live corpus …

**L13 · indexing-v3-agent request loop: an unguarded `markJobFailure` in the catch path strands the rest of the claimed batch; `limit` NaN and GET are accepted** — `supabase/functions/indexing-v3-agent/index.ts:1914, 1921, 1948-1953, 1774` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L05-F5

In the per-job loop the catch calls `await markJobFailure(job, msg)` (1952) with no guard. `markJobFailure` -> `updateAgentJobStatus` throws when the RPC returns `ok:false` (1774) or on any DB error, which escapes to the outer catch and returns 500, leaving every not-yet-processed job in the batch `processing` with its lock until the 45-minute stale reclaim, each burning one of `max_attempts`. Separately `limit` is parsed as `Number(...)` with no NaN guard (1921: `?limit=abc` -> `NaN::integer` -> Postgres cast error -> 500 before any claim) and GET is accepted for a mutating endpoint (1914). **Impact:** Up to 49 enrichment jobs delayed 45 minutes and one attempt closer to the terminal `failed` state that #W98GR7 notes is never re-queued. **Fix:** Wrap `markJobFailure` in its own try/catch that logs and continues; parse `limit` with `Number.isFinite` fallback like the ingestion-worker function does; reject GET. **Skeptic:** All three sub-claims are literal in the code; the batch-stranding consequence follows from claim_indexing_v3_agent_jobs' 45-minute stale reclaim (schema.sql:1195 `p_stale_after_minutes integer default 45`). The precondition (an RPC ok:false or DB error while recording the first failure of a multi-job batch) is transient, not exotic. Low: …

**L14 · Freshness stamps print wall-clock times with no timezone; server-rendered stamps use the container clock (UTC on Railway) while the ingestion panel's stamp uses the browser, so one page shows times eight hours apart for a Perth reader** — `src/components/developer-area/hub/freshness-stamp.tsx:7-11; src/components/developer-area/hub/ingestion-panel.tsx:191-195` · correctness · PLAUSIBLE · safe_cleanup=false · L10-F7

`FreshnessStamp` is a Server Component (no `"use client"` in panel-page-shell.tsx or freshness-stamp.tsx) and formats `viewedAt`/`contentAt` with `toLocaleString("en-AU", { dateStyle, timeStyle })` — no `timeZone`, no zone name — so the output follows the server's TZ. Neither `Dockerfile` nor the Railway config sets `TZ` (rg 'TZ' Dockerfile railway.* -> no hits), so production prints UTC. `CheckedAt` in the client `IngestionPanel` formats with the browser clock (Perth). On /mockups/development/ingestion both appear together. **Impact:** The stamps exist so a reader can judge how stale a number is; an unlabelled 8-hour discrepancy defeats that on the one page that polls live. **Fix:** Pass `timeZone: "Australia/Perth", timeZoneName: "short"` in both formatters (or set `TZ=Australia/Perth` on the Railway service and still print the zone name). **Skeptic:** Code is as described and both stamps share one page. The eight-hour split depends on the production container's clock being UTC, which cannot be established offline (Railway default is normally UTC but nothing in the repo pins it) — hence PLAUSIBLE. Admin-only diagnostic page, no clinical figure: Low.

**L15 · Ingestion panel's 'failed jobs' tile counts only the first page of results while 'active jobs' is the server's full count** — `src/components/developer-area/hub/ingestion-panel.tsx:373-375` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L10-F10

`<CountTile value={failed.length} label="failed jobs" />` derives from `bucketJobs(state.jobs)`, i.e. the page the endpoint returned (default `limit` 100, newest first), whereas `activeJobCount` is a pre-pagination `head: true` count. The 'more' note says 'Showing the first N of M jobs' but the failed tile is not labelled per-page, and the empty-state copy ('No jobs have failed on this page') shows the author knew the distinction. With >100 jobs, older failures are silently excluded from the number. **Impact:** Under-reports failures — the direction the panel's own doc comment says it must not fail in ('what keeps failing' is one of the two facts the page exists to answer). **Fix:** Relabel the tile 'failed on this page' or add a `failedJobCount` head-count to the endpoint (mirroring `activeCountQuery` with `.eq("status", "failed")`) and render that. **Skeptic:** The tile's label is total-sounding while its value is page-scoped; older failures beyond the newest 100 rows vanish from the number. Admin ingestion dashboard, not clinical output: Low. Fix (relabel or add a `failedJobCount` head-count) is local.

**L17 · Client stream collapses 401/429 into 'internal_error', so the rate_limited and unauthorized codes in the contract are never shown** — `src/lib/clinical-ask/client-stream.ts:32; src/lib/clinical-ask/contracts.ts:70-82` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L12-F5

`if (!response.ok || !response.body) return failedPayload(request, "internal_error")` treats the JSON 429 from `rateLimitJsonResponse` and the 401 from `unauthorizedResponse` the same as a network fault, discarding `retryAfterSeconds` and the server message. The contract's `rate_limited`/`unauthorized` codes exist only in the schema. **Impact:** The user sees 'Clinical Ask stream could not be read.' with a retryable flag instead of 'try again in N seconds' or 'sign in', which invites immediate retries that keep hitting the limiter. **Fix:** Branch on `response.status` 401/429 and map to the existing `unauthorized`/`rate_limited` codes, carrying the server message and Retry-After into the failed payload. **Skeptic:** Straight code trace; the 429 and 401 collapse into a generic retryable failure. Whether WA Health serves clinical pages on a subhost was not verifiable offline. **Fix:** Decide the intended policy: either allow `*.domain` with a registry `allowedHosts` list, or keep exact hosts and pass the exact hostnames to the provider; add a subdomain test either way and correct the governance sentence (path prefixes are not implemented). **Skeptic:** Exact-host matching and the missing path-prefix field/subdomain test are confirmed from code and docs. **L21 · Indexing-stamp query errors collapse to the same cache key as an empty corpus, so a transient outage can pin a stale answer across a reindex** — `src/lib/rag/rag-cache.ts:509-511 (`if (error || !data?.length) value = ...:no-indexed-documents`), :488/:524 (unavailable stamp), :262-283 (setCachedAnswer writ` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L22-F4

`cacheIndexingVersion` is the corpus-staleness guard for both local and shared answer/search caches. A PostgREST error on the `documents` stamp query is folded into the same constant as a genuinely empty corpus (`no-indexed-documents`), cached for 5 s, and then used as the `indexing_version` for writes (`setCachedAnswer`, `setCachedSearch`, `replaceSharedCacheRow`) and reads (`sharedCacheSelector`). Unlike the thrown-exception path (:524, `index-stamp-unavailable`), the error-object path is indistinguishable from 'nothing indexed', and neither path refuses to write. **Impact:** An answer generated before a document change can be served with `answer_cache_hit` after the change, bypassing the guard that exists precisely to prevent that. Requires a double fault, so probability is low; consequence is a stale clinical answer with a clean-looking routing reason. **Fix:** Map `error` to the `index-stamp-unavailable` constant (distinct from empty corpus) and make `setCachedAnswer`/`setCachedSearch` return early when the stamp ends with `index-stamp-unavailable`; add a unit test in tests/rag-cache-invalidation.test.ts for the error branch. **L22 · Clinical hazard-controls gate escapes control symbols with a malformed regex, so metacharacters are not escaped** — `scripts/check-clinical-hazard-controls.mjs:177` · correctness · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=true · SEM-regex-redos-F1

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
The symbol-presence check builds `new RegExp(`\\b${String(symbol).replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\]/g, "\\$&")}\\b`)`. The escape regex `/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\]/g`is mis-written: JavaScript parses it as the character class`[.*+?^${}()|[\\]` FOLLOWED BY the literal sequence `\]`, i.e. it only matches three-character runs such as `.\]`, so in practice no metacharacter in a control symbol is ever escaped. Every other script in the repo uses the correct `/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g`(for example scripts/pr-policy.mjs:227, scripts/check-dead-code-candidate.mjs:189). **Impact:** The gate that proves each clinical hazard's named control still exists in its control files becomes unreliable for non-identifier symbols: a`.`acts as a wildcard (fail-open — the symbol is reported present when it is not), while`$`, `(` or `[` either never match (a spurious `control symbol ... … **Fix:** Replace the inline escape with the repo's standard `String(symbol).replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, "\\$&")` (or import a shared escapeRegExp helper), and add one unit case with a dotted symbol such as `foo.bar` asserting it does not match `fooxbar`. Run `npm run test:focused -- --files … **Skeptic:** The mis-parsed character class is proven: the escape is a no-op for every metacharacter, so a dotted symbol acts as a wildcard (fail-open) and `(`/`[`/`$` symbols either never match or throw. Today's 13 symbols are plain identifiers so the gate currently behaves correctly — the bug is latent, which with the developer-gate-only blast radius keeps …

**L26 · npm run clean:artifacts deletes a tracked repository file: docs/archive/staging-tenancy-evidence-29795051547/ is git-tracked but matched by the script's rimraf glob and by .gitignore** — `package.json:263 (clean:artifacts); .gitignore:167; docs/archive/staging-tenancy-evidence-29795051547/staging-tenancy-evidence.json` · data-integrity · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L15-F5

`clean:artifacts` is `npx rimraf test-output*.txt docs/archive/staging-tenancy-evidence-*`. rimraf 6.1.3 does not expand globs itself, but npm runs the line through sh, which expands `docs/archive/staging-tenancy-evidence-*` to the tracked directory; rimraf then removes it recursively. No doc or script references the directory. **Impact:** A tracked evidence document disappears from the working tree and shows up as an unexpected deletion in the next commit; because the path is ignored, `git status` porcelain still reports it (tracked files ignore .gitignore) but re-adding it later needs `git add -f`. The readiness endpoint (Railway healthcheck target, so it cannot be gated) executes `select id from import_batches limit 1` through the service-role client for any internet caller with no rate limit. **L109 · answerSentenceFragments regex is quadratic on long digit runs in answer text** — `src/lib/rag/rag-answer-text.ts:192` · performance · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · SEM-shell-docker-secrets-F2

`value.match(/(?:\d+\.\d+|[^.!?])+[.!?]?/g)` has overlapping alternatives (`\d+\.\d+` and `[^.!?]` both consume digits), so on a run of digits with no decimal point the engine retries `\d+` from every position before falling back to the character branch — O(n^2). Note: this row's check_id is detect-redos, outside this family's regex; it was examined because the task named it explicitly, so the ReDoS lane may dedupe it. **Impact:** Bounded latency cost on the answer route (and on the browser during display polishing) rather than an outage; an extreme 50k-digit answer would add ~1 s of synchronous CPU. No correctness or data effect. **Fix:** Make the alternatives disjoint, e.g. split on sentence terminators while protecting decimals: `value.match(/(?:\d+\.\d+|[^.!?\d]|\d)+[.!?]?/g)` still overlaps; better is a small manual scanner, or first cap the input (`value.length > 20000 ? value.slice(0, 20000)`) before matching. Any change here … **Skeptic:** Quadratic behaviour reproduces offline and is worse than the finder measured on this box (2.5 s at 50k digits). Input is bounded by model output tokens and chunk size, so it is a bounded synchronous CPU cost on the answer route, not an outage or wrong number — Low performance. The finder's client-path reference is wrong (no such file), but the …

The route returns either the full diagnostic payload (operator token or admin bearer) or the coarse payload, under `Cache-Control: private, max-age=5, stale-while-revalidate=30` with no `Vary: Authorization, x-health-deep-token`. **Impact:** Same-user only (`private`), so no cross-user leak; the polled setup UI can briefly show the wrong detail level. Low. Adding Vary or no-store for the detailed variant is safe.

conciseSourceText() returns `normalized.slice(0, 257).trim() + '...'` with no word/number boundary, so a dose or count straddling index 257 is rendered partially. **Impact:** Chip shows e.g. 'ANC 15...' for 'ANC 1500'; the ellipsis signals truncation but the partial number is visible in a Safety finding. **Fix:** Cut at the last whitespace before 257 (and never inside `\d`), or at sentence end. **Skeptic:** The quoted lines cut at a fixed character offset with no word/number boundary, so a numeric token straddling index 257 is rendered partially before the ellipsis. (b) `buildClinicalAskTelemetry`/`clinicalAskLatencyBucket` have no importer outside tests (knip export log line 201 flags `clinicalAskLatencyBucket`); the content-free telemetry the governance doc describes is never emitted. (c) `priorTurns` (up to 6 x 2,000 chars) is validated and accepted but never read by the orchestrator or synthesis, and `containsIdentifier`/`identifierInput` do not scan it — harmless today, a leak trap if someone … **Impact:** Maintenance noise; (b) means operators have no Clinical Ask outcome metrics despite docs implying them; (c) is a latent privacy trap. **Fix:** Drop line 96; either wire telemetry into the route's `finally` (content-free fields only) or record the module as a triage candidate; either remove priorTurns from the request contract or include it in the identifier scan now. **Skeptic:** All three sub-claims verified from code and the pre-gathered knip evidence. Dead branch, unwired telemetry, and a latent (not live) privacy trap: Low. Dropping line 96 or the telemetry export must respect docs/agents/dead-code-deletion.md; adding priorTurns to the identifier scan is additive and safe.

**L129 · Overrides block carries an entry that matches nothing and two exact pins with no recorded rationale; the probe's '9 overrides' is actually 10 keys** — `package.json overrides (postcss, esbuild, sharp, tmp, uuid, brace-expansion@1, brace-expansion@2, brace-expansion@5, exceljs.{archiver,unzipper})` · quality/simplification · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=true · L17-F4

`brace-expansion@2: ^2.1.4` matches no package: the lock contains brace-expansion 1.1.18 (×1) and 5.0.9 (×3) only. `esbuild: 0.28.2` duplicates the devDependency pin (tsx wants ~0.28.0, vite ^0.27||^0.28 — no override needed to converge). `sharp: 0.35.3` equals next's own `^0.35.3` floor but freezes it as exact, so a next patch that raises the sharp floor would conflict at install time. Only postcss/tmp/uuid have a written justification (docs/archive/project-alignment-cleanup.md:11). The nested `exceljs → archiver ^8.0.0 / unzipper ^0.12.5` overrides force two majors above exceljs 4.4.0's … **Impact:** Repository hygiene: dead override lines make the block look reviewed when it is not, and an exact `sharp` pin can turn a routine Next patch into an install conflict on Railway. **Fix:** Drop `brace-expansion@2` and the redundant `esbuild` override; either relax `sharp` to `^0.35.3` or add a one-line comment/README note per remaining override (why it exists, which advisory it closes, when it can go); add a focused XLSX extraction fixture test if none exists. **Skeptic:** Dead `brace-expansion@2` override and redundant esbuild pin reproduce; the exact sharp pin is a real install-conflict risk on a Next patch. The 'no offline XLSX test' caveat is refuted (an exceljs round-trip test exists), which slightly narrows the finding but does not remove it. Hygiene, Low. Fix is a manifest edit; run check:npm-ci-dry-run after.

**L132 · .cursorignore starts with a UTF-8 BOM, contrary to .editorconfig charset=utf-8** — `.cursorignore:1 (bytes EF BB BF before '# Cursor exclusions…')` · quality/simplification · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=true · L17-F8

**REMEDIATED (2026-09-30 tip).** `.cursorignore` has no UTF-8 BOM (starts with `#`). Historical narrative below kept for evidence; do not reopen.
The file has carried a BOM since #259 (2026-07-05). Its first line is a comment, so the only pattern that could be corrupted is a comment — no exclusion is affected today — but a future edit that moves a real pattern to line 1 would silently not match, and the BOM contradicts .editorconfig's `charset = utf-8` (BOM-less). **Impact:** Hygiene only; potential silent loss of one Cursor exclusion (e.g. `.env*`) if the layout changes. **Fix:** Re-save the file without a BOM (one-byte-level change, zero behaviour change). **Skeptic:** BOM present as claimed; line 1 is a comment so no exclusion is affected today. Hygiene-only, Low; the one-byte fix is behaviour-neutral.

**L133 · `ledger:lookup` prints every record path on one line, producing ~60 KB per lookup** — `scripts/branch-review-ledger.mjs (lookup output, `files:` line)` · quality/simplification · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L19-F9

A single `npm run ledger:lookup -- <ref>` emits a `files:` line listing all 580+ record paths before the verdict; the orchestrator already has to rule `| tail -3` to keep it usable, and the review-throttle instruction in docs/agents/codex-review-throttling.md tells every reviewer to run it first. **Impact:** Context blow-out for agents that must run it before every review; the decisive line (`NOT REVIEWED at this HEAD.`) is the last one. **Fix:** Print a count (`files: 2 ledgers + 579 records`) and move the full list behind `--verbose`. **Skeptic:** Traced: every lookup prints all ~580 content-addressed record paths on one line ahead of the decisive verdict. Low quality issue; the finder's own tool-result size (62 KB after `| tail -3`) is consistent with a single very long line. Fix (count + `--verbose`) does not touch record content, the ledger, or any gate; `--json` output could keep the …

**L134 · Process-local answer cache evicts by insertion order and overwrites keep their old position, so a refreshed hot answer is evicted first** — `src/lib/rag/rag-cache.ts:264-275` · quality/simplification · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=false · L22-F7

`answerCache.set(key, …)` without a preceding `delete` leaves a re-cached key at its original Map position, and `getCachedAnswer` never bumps recency; eviction `while (size > RAG_ANSWER_CACHE_SIZE)` therefore drops the oldest-inserted entry, not the least-recently used. `owner-catalogue-cache.ts:48-49,61` (module deleted 2026-09-19, issue `#BDJWAH`) and `signed-url-cache.ts:41-42,50` implement the delete-then-set LRU bump correctly for the same shape, and `bounded-ttl-cache.ts:25` does too — this is the one cache in the lane that does not. **Impact:** Lower hit-rate than the cache size suggests on a ward-round pattern (the same handful of questions repeated); `cacheMetricsSnapshot` would under-report effectiveness. No correctness impact. **Fix:** Route answerCache through `writeBoundedExpiringCacheEntry` from bounded-ttl-cache.ts (already imported at :4) or add the delete-before-set and a read-side bump. RAG impact: no retrieval behaviour change. **Skeptic:** FIFO-not-LRU is confirmed: hits never bump recency, so a hot repeated question ages out in insertion order. The 'overwrites keep their old position' half is largely unreachable — getCachedAnswer deletes the key on expiry or version mismatch before a miss (rag-cache.ts:208-215), so a re-set normally appends at the end; only a concurrent duplicate …

**L135 · Colour-coding reference heading has a dead isGuide ternary (both branches identical)** — `src/components/reference/colour-coding-reference-content.tsx:101` · quality/simplification · CONFIRMED · safe_cleanup=true · SEM-shell-docker-secrets-F1

`isGuide ? "text-2xl sm:text-3xl" : "text-2xl sm:text-3xl"` selects the same class string in both branches, so the heading size never varies by mode although the adjacent eyebrow, tabIndex and data-guide-page-heading props do. Either the Guide Centre and standalone reference views were meant to differ in heading scale (and that intent was lost when the page moved into the Guide Centre in PR #2310, which introduced the line), or the ternary is dead code that misleads the next editor. **Impact:** No clinical or data impact; visual/maintenance only. A future editor changing one branch expecting a mode-specific effect would be surprised; the semgrep useless-ternary ERROR-severity rule also fires on it in every scan. **Fix:** Replace the ternary with the literal `"text-2xl sm:text-3xl"` (or, if the Guide Centre heading was meant to be larger, make the isGuide branch differ). One-line change; no test changes needed. **Skeptic:** Line reads exactly as claimed; both branches are the identical literal so the ternary is dead. Purely cosmetic/maintenance; no clinical, data or gate consequence — Low. One-line fix; the lucide/type-scale ESLint rules are indifferent to it, and no test pins the heading class.
